About The Academic

The Academic is a comprehensive resource for scholars, researchers, and students navigating the complex landscape of modern academia. Our mission is to provide actionable insights, proven methodologies, and cutting-edge approaches to enhance your scholarly work.

From strategic thinking and classic translations, we provide deep insights and a cross-linguistic bridge, helping readers understand the principles and historical context behind strategic thinking while broadening their perspectives.

AI&Philosophy January 15, 2026

Tao Te Ching (Standard Received Version)

All eighty-one chapters of the Tao Te Ching in bilingual layout — English on the left, Chinese on the right — with an editable text option for readers.

By Laozi
Chapter 1 · 第一章

The way that can be spoken of is not the eternal Way; the name that can be named is not the eternal Name. The nameless is the origin of heaven and earth; the named is the mother of all things. Therefore, constantly free of desire, one observes its mysteries; constantly filled with desire, one observes its manifestations. These two spring from the same source but differ in name; together they are called the mystery. The mystery of mysteries is the gateway to all wonders.

道可道，非常道。名可名，非常名。无名天地之始；有名万物之母。故常无欲，以观其妙；常有欲，以观其徼。此两者，同出而异名，同谓之玄。玄之又玄，众妙之门。

Chapter 2 · 第二章

When all under heaven know beauty as beauty, ugliness arises; when all know good as good, evil arises. Thus being and non-being produce each other; difficult and easy complete each other; long and short contrast each other; high and low incline toward each other; sound and voice harmonize with each other; before and after follow each other. Therefore the sage acts without action and teaches without words. All things arise, and he does not reject them; he gives them life without possessing them; he acts without claiming merit; he accomplishes without dwelling on it. Precisely because he does not dwell on it, his merit never leaves him.

天下皆知美之为美，斯恶已；皆知善之为善，斯不善已。故有无相生，难易相成，长短相形，高下相倾，音声相和，前后相随。是以圣人处无为之事，行不言之教。万物作焉而不辞，生而不有，为而不恃，功成而弗居。夫唯弗居，是以不去。

Chapter 3 · 第三章

Do not exalt the worthy, and the people will not compete. Do not prize rare goods, and the people will not steal. Do not display what is desirable, and the people's hearts will not be disturbed. Thus the sage governs by emptying their hearts, filling their bellies, weakening their ambitions, and strengthening their bones. He keeps the people free of knowledge and desire, so that the clever dare not act. Act without action, and nothing is left ungoverned.

不尚贤，使民不争；不贵难得之货，使民不为盗；不见可欲，使心不乱。是以圣人之治，虚其心，实其腹，弱其志，强其骨。常使民无知无欲，使夫智者不敢为也。为无为，则无不治。

Chapter 4 · 第四章

The Way is empty, yet when used it is never exhausted. Bottomless, it seems the ancestor of all things. It blunts the sharp, unties the tangled, softens the glare, and merges with the dust. Hidden, it seems barely present. I do not know whose child it is; it appears to precede the Lord of Heaven.

道冲，而用之或不盈。渊兮似万物之宗。挫其锐，解其纷，和其光，同其尘。湛兮似或存。吾不知谁之子，象帝之先。

Chapter 5 · 第五章

Heaven and earth are not benevolent; they treat all things as straw dogs. The sage is not benevolent; he treats the people as straw dogs. Between heaven and earth, is it not like a bellows? Empty yet inexhaustible, the more it moves, the more it produces. Many words lead to exhaustion; better to hold to the center.

天地不仁，以万物为刍狗；圣人不仁，以百姓为刍狗。天地之间，其犹橐籥乎？虚而不屈，动而愈出。多言数穷，不如守中。

Chapter 6 · 第六章

The valley spirit never dies; it is called the mysterious feminine. The gate of the mysterious feminine is the root of heaven and earth. Continuous and unbroken, its use is never worn out.

谷神不死，是谓玄牝。玄牝之门，是谓天地根。绵绵若存，用之不勤。

Chapter 7 · 第七章

Heaven is enduring and earth is lasting. Heaven and earth can endure because they do not live for themselves; thus they can live long. Therefore the sage places himself behind, yet comes first; treats himself as outside, yet endures. Is it not because he has no self-interest that his self-interest is fulfilled?

天长地久。天地所以能长且久者，以其不自生，故能长生。是以圣人后其身而身先，外其身而身存。非以其无私邪？故能成其私。

Chapter 8 · 第八章

The highest good is like water. Water benefits all things without contending, and settles where others disdain; thus it is close to the Way. In dwelling, choose the ground; in mind, choose depth; in association, choose benevolence; in words, choose trust; in government, choose order; in action, choose ability; in timing, choose the moment. Because he does not contend, he is beyond reproach.

上善若水。水善利万物而不争，处众人之所恶，故几于道。居善地，心善渊，与善仁，言善信，正善治，事善能，动善时。夫唯不争，故无尤。

Chapter 9 · 第九章

Rather than fill a vessel to overflowing, stop in time. Sharpen an edge too much, and it cannot be kept long. A hall filled with gold and jade cannot be guarded. Wealth and honor with arrogance invite disaster. Withdraw when the work is done — this is the Way of heaven.

持而盈之，不如其已；揣而锐之，不可长保。金玉满堂，莫之能守；富贵而骄，自遗其咎。功遂身退，天之道也。

Chapter 10 · 第十章

Can you carry body and soul together without separation? Can you concentrate your breath to become as soft as a newborn child? Can you cleanse your inner mirror so it is without blemish? Can you love the people and govern the land without action? Can you open and close the gates of heaven like the feminine? Can you understand all things without knowledge? It gives life and nurtures; it gives life without possessing; it acts without claiming; it raises without dominating. This is the profound virtue.

载营魄抱一，能无离乎？专气致柔，能如婴儿乎？涤除玄览，能无疵乎？爱民治国，能无为乎？天门开阖，能为雌乎？明白四达，能无知乎？生之畜之，生而不有，为而不恃，长而不宰，是谓玄德。

Chapter 11 · 第十一章

Thirty spokes share one hub; in its emptiness lies the usefulness of the wheel. Clay is shaped into a vessel; in its emptiness lies the usefulness of the vessel. Doors and windows are cut to make a room; in their emptiness lies the usefulness of the room. Therefore, being provides advantage, but non-being provides usefulness.

三十辐共一毂，当其无，有车之用。埏埴以为器，当其无，有器之用。凿户牖以为室，当其无，有室之用。故有之以为利，无之以为用。

Chapter 12 · 第十二章

The five colors blind the eye; the five tones deafen the ear; the five flavors dull the palate; racing and hunting madden the mind; rare goods lead astray. Therefore the sage is for the belly, not for the eye; he rejects the one and chooses the other.

五色令人目盲；五音令人耳聋；五味令人口爽；驰骋畋猎令人心发狂；难得之货令人行妨。是以圣人为腹不为目，故去彼取此。

Chapter 13 · 第十三章

Favor and disgrace are alike a cause for alarm; value great troubles as you value your body. What does it mean that favor and disgrace are alike a cause for alarm? Favor lifts one up; gaining it is alarming, losing it is alarming. What does it mean that great troubles are valued as the body? If I had no body, what trouble could I have? Therefore, one who values the world as his body may be entrusted with the world; one who loves the world as himself may be given the world.

宠辱若惊，贵大患若身。何谓宠辱若惊？宠为下，得之若惊，失之若惊，是谓宠辱若惊。何谓贵大患若身？吾所以有大患者，为吾有身，及吾无身，吾有何患？故贵以身为天下，若可寄天下；爱以身为天下，若可托天下。

Chapter 14 · 第十四章

Looked at but unseen, it is called the invisible; listened to but unheard, it is called the inaudible; grasped but untouched, it is called the subtle. These three cannot be fully grasped, so they merge into one. Its rising is not bright, its setting is not dark; endless and unnameable, it returns to nothingness. This is the formless form, the imageless image, called the elusive. Meet it and you see no face; follow it and you see no back. Hold to the ancient Way to master the present; knowing the ancient beginning is the thread of the Way.

视之不见，名曰夷；听之不闻，名曰希；搏之不得，名曰微。此三者不可致诘，故混而为一。其上不皦，其下不昧，绳绳兮不可名，复归于无物。是谓无状之状，无物之象，是谓惚恍。迎之不见其首，随之不见其后。执古之道，以御今之有。能知古始，是谓道纪。

Chapter 15 · 第十五章

The ancient masters were subtle, mysterious, and profound beyond understanding. Because they cannot be understood, one can only describe them: cautious, as if crossing a frozen stream; hesitant, as if fearing neighbors; reserved, as if guests; yielding, as melting ice; simple, as uncarved wood; open, as a valley; murky, as muddy water. Who can remain muddy until it slowly clears? Who can remain still until life slowly stirs? Those who keep this Way do not seek fullness; not being full, they can be renewed and endure.

古之善为士者，微妙玄通，深不可识。夫唯不可识，故强为之容：豫兮若冬涉川；犹兮若畏四邻；俨兮其若客；涣兮若冰之将释；敦兮其若朴；旷兮其若谷；混兮其若浊。孰能浊以静之徐清？孰能安以久动之徐生？保此道者，不欲盈。夫唯不盈，故能蔽不新成。

Chapter 16 · 第十六章

Reach the utmost emptiness and guard perfect stillness. All things arise together; I watch them return. Each thing flourishes and returns to its root. Returning to the root is stillness; this is called fulfilling life. Fulfilling life is the constant; knowing the constant is enlightenment. Not knowing the constant, one acts recklessly and invites misfortune. Knowing the constant brings tolerance; tolerance brings impartiality; impartiality brings kingship; kingship draws near heaven; heaven draws near the Way; abiding in the Way, one endures to the end of life without peril.

致虚极，守静笃。万物并作，吾以观复。夫物芸芸，各复归其根。归根曰静，是谓复命。复命曰常，知常曰明。不知常，妄作凶。知常容，容乃公，公乃王，王乃天，天乃道，道乃久，没身不殆。

Chapter 17 · 第十七章

The best ruler is barely known to exist; the next is loved and praised; the next is feared; the worst is despised. When trust is insufficient, distrust follows. The sage values few words; when work is done and affairs succeed, the people all say, "It happened naturally."

太上，不知有之；其次，亲而誉之；其次，畏之；其次，侮之。信不足焉，有不信焉。悠兮其贵言。功成事遂，百姓皆谓我自然。

Chapter 18 · 第十八章

When the great Way is abandoned, benevolence and righteousness appear. When cleverness and wisdom emerge, great hypocrisy follows. When the six family relations fall into discord, filial piety and parental affection arise. When the state is in chaos, loyal ministers appear.

大道废，有仁义；慧智出，有大伪；六亲不和，有孝慈；国家昏乱，有忠臣。

Chapter 19 · 第十九章

Abandon sagehood and discard wisdom, and the people benefit a hundredfold. Abandon benevolence and discard righteousness, and the people return to filial piety. Abandon craft and discard profit, and robbers disappear. These three are adornments and not enough; therefore let there be something to rely on: embrace simplicity, reduce selfishness, lessen desires, abandon learning, and be free of worry.

绝圣弃智，民利百倍；绝仁弃义，民复孝慈；绝巧弃利，盗贼无有。此三者以为文，不足。故令有所属：见素抱朴，少私寡欲，绝学无忧。

Chapter 20 · 第二十章

Between "yes" and "well, of course," how much difference is there? Between good and evil, how much? What others fear, one cannot but fear — how endless it all is! Most people are joyful, as if feasting at a great sacrifice or climbing a tower in spring. I alone am still, without sign, like an infant who has not yet smiled; listless, as if with no home. Others have more than enough; I alone seem to lack. Mine is the mind of a fool — so plain! Others are bright; I alone am dim. Others are sharp; I alone am dull, like the tranquil sea. Others have purpose; I alone am stubborn and lowly. I alone differ from others, and value the nourishing mother.

唯之与阿，相去几何？善之与恶，相去若何？人之所畏，不可不畏。荒兮其未央哉！众人熙熙，如享太牢，如春登台。我独泊兮其未兆，如婴儿之未孩；傫傫兮若无所归。众人皆有余，而我独若遗。我愚人之心也哉！沌沌兮，俗人昭昭，我独昏昏。俗人察察，我独闷闷。澹兮其若海，飂兮若无止。众人皆有以，而我独顽似鄙。我独异于人，而贵食母。

Chapter 21 · 第二十一章

The form of great virtue follows only the Way. The Way as a thing is elusive and vague. Vague and elusive, within it is an image; elusive and vague, within it is a substance; dim and dark, within it is essence. Its essence is most real; within it is faith. From ancient times to now, its name has not departed; through it one can survey the origin of all things. How do I know the state of all origins? By this.

孔德之容，惟道是从。道之为物，惟恍惟惚。惚兮恍兮，其中有象；恍兮惚兮，其中有物。窈兮冥兮，其中有精；其精甚真，其中有信。自古及今，其名不去，以阅众甫。吾何以知众甫之状哉？以此。

Chapter 22 · 第二十二章

Yield and you remain whole; bend and you straighten; hollow and you fill; worn and you renew; have little and you gain; have much and you are lost. Therefore the sage embraces the One and becomes a model for the world. Not displaying himself, he shines; not asserting himself, he is distinguished; not claiming merit, he succeeds; not boasting, he endures. Because he does not contend, none in the world can contend with him. The ancient saying "yield and remain whole" — is it empty words? Truly, it brings one back to wholeness.

曲则全，枉则直，洼则盈，敝则新，少则得，多则惑。是以圣人抱一为天下式。不自见，故明；不自是，故彰；不自伐，故有功；不自矜，故长。夫唯不争，故天下莫能与之争。古之所谓曲则全者，岂虚言哉！诚全而归之。

Chapter 23 · 第二十三章

To speak little is natural. A whirlwind does not last a morning; a downpour does not last a day. Who makes them? Heaven and earth. If heaven and earth cannot last long, how much less can man? Therefore, one who follows the Way is one with the Way; one who follows virtue is one with virtue; one who loses them is one with the loss. Where trust is lacking, there is no trust.

希言自然。故飘风不终朝，骤雨不终日。孰为此者？天地。天地尚不能久，而况于人乎？故从事于道者，道者同于道，德者同于德，失者同于失。同于道者，道亦乐得之；同于德者，德亦乐得之；同于失者，失亦乐得之。信不足焉，有不信焉。

Chapter 24 · 第二十四章

Those on tiptoe cannot stand; those with legs astride cannot walk. Those who see themselves are not illuminated; those who assert themselves are not distinguished; those who boast have no merit; those who are proud do not endure. In the Way, these are like leftover food and excess growth — all things detest them, so those who have the Way do not dwell on them.

企者不立；跨者不行；自见者不明；自是者不彰；自伐者无功；自矜者不长。其在道也，曰：余食赘形。物或恶之，故有道者不处。

Chapter 25 · 第二十五章

There was a thing formed in chaos, born before heaven and earth. Silent and empty, standing alone and unchanging, circling without tiring — it can be the mother of heaven and earth. I do not know its name; I call it the Way, and force on it the name "great." Great means passing on; passing on means reaching far; reaching far means returning. Therefore the Way is great, heaven is great, earth is great, and man is also great. Within the realm there are four greats, and man is one of them. Man follows earth; earth follows heaven; heaven follows the Way; the Way follows what is natural.

有物混成，先天地生。寂兮寥兮，独立而不改，周行而不殆，可以为天地母。吾不知其名，强字之曰道，强为之名曰大。大曰逝，逝曰远，远曰反。故道大，天大，地大，人亦大。域中有四大，而人居其一焉。人法地，地法天，天法道，道法自然。

Chapter 26 · 第二十六章

Heaviness is the root of lightness; stillness is the master of haste. Therefore the sage travels all day without leaving his baggage; though surrounded by splendor, he remains calm. How can the lord of ten thousand chariots treat his own body lightly? To be light is to lose the root; to be hasty is to lose the master.

重为轻根，静为躁君。是以君子终日行不离辎重。虽有荣观，燕处超然。奈何万乘之主，而以身轻天下？轻则失根，躁则失君。

Chapter 27 · 第二十七章

Good traveling leaves no tracks; good words leave no flaws; good counting needs no tally; good closing needs no bolt, yet cannot be opened; good binding needs no rope, yet cannot be untied. Therefore the sage is always good at saving people, so no one is abandoned; always good at saving things, so nothing is wasted. This is called hidden illumination. So the good are teachers of the not-good, and the not-good are material for the good. Not valuing the teacher, not loving the material — though clever, one is greatly astray. This is the essential mystery.

善行无辙迹；善言无瑕谪；善数不用筹策；善闭无关楗而不可开；善结无绳约而不可解。是以圣人常善救人，故无弃人；常善救物，故无弃物。是谓袭明。故善人者，不善人之师；不善人者，善人之资。不贵其师，不爱其资，虽智大迷，是谓要妙。

Chapter 28 · 第二十八章

Know the masculine, keep to the feminine, and become the valley of the world. Being the valley of the world, constant virtue never departs; return to the innocence of a child. Know the white, keep to the black, and become the model of the world. Being the model, constant virtue does not err; return to the limitless. Know glory, keep to disgrace, and become the valley of the world. Being the valley, constant virtue is full; return to simplicity. When simplicity is dispersed, it becomes tools, and the sage uses them to govern. Thus the greatest carving cuts nothing.

知其雄，守其雌，为天下溪。为天下溪，常德不离，复归于婴儿。知其白，守其黑，为天下式。为天下式，常德不忒，复归于无极。知其荣，守其辱，为天下谷。为天下谷，常德乃足，复归于朴。朴散则为器，圣人用之，则为官长。故大制不割。

Chapter 29 · 第二十九章

One who would take the world and shape it will fail — I see that it cannot be done. The world is a sacred vessel; it cannot be shaped or grasped. To shape is to ruin; to grasp is to lose. Therefore the sage does not shape, so nothing is ruined; does not grasp, so nothing is lost. Some lead, some follow; some blow hot, some cold; some grow strong, some weak; some rise, some fall. Therefore the sage discards excess, extravagance, and extremes.

将欲取天下而为之，吾见其不得已。天下神器，不可为也，不可执也。为者败之，执者失之。是以圣人无为，故无败；无执，故无失。夫物或行或随；或歔或吹；或强或羸；或载或隳。是以圣人去甚，去奢，去泰。

Chapter 30 · 第三十章

One who assists the ruler with the Way does not use weapons to dominate the world; such deeds rebound. Where armies camp, thorns grow; after great wars come years of famine. The good achieve their aim and stop; they dare not rely on force. Achieving, they do not boast; do not claim; do not grow arrogant; act only when forced; do not press the advantage. Things that grow strong grow old — this is not the Way; what is not the Way ends early.

以道佐人主者，不以兵强天下。其事好还。师之所处，荆棘生焉。大军之后，必有凶年。善者果而已，不敢以取强。果而勿矜，果而勿伐，果而勿骄，果而不得已，果而勿强。物壮则老，是谓不道，不道早已。

Chapter 31 · 第三十一章

Weapons are instruments of ill omen; all things detest them, so those with the Way do not rest on them. At home the gentleman honors the left; in war the right. Weapons are not the gentleman's tools; only when unavoidable does he use them, and calm is best. Victory is no glory; to glorify it is to delight in killing. One who delights in killing cannot gain the world's trust. Auspicious affairs favor the left, inauspicious the right; mourning rites govern the aftermath of battle, and the killing is mourned with tears.

夫兵者，不祥之器，物或恶之，故有道者不处。君子居则贵左，用兵则贵右。兵者不祥之器，非君子之器，不得已而用之，恬淡为上。胜而不美，而美之者，是乐杀人。夫乐杀人者，则不可得志于天下矣。吉事尚左，凶事尚右。偏将军居左，上将军居右，言以丧礼处之。杀人之众，以悲哀泣之，战胜以丧礼处之。

Chapter 32 · 第三十二章

The Way is eternally nameless; simple and unadorned, though small, none in the world can master it. If rulers could keep it, all things would follow of themselves. Heaven and earth would unite, sweet dew would fall, and the people would be equally at ease without command. Once names are distinguished, know also when to stop; knowing when to stop avoids danger. The Way in the world is like rivers and streams flowing to the sea.

道常无名，朴。虽小，天下莫能臣。侯王若能守之，万物将自宾。天地相合，以降甘露，民莫之令而自均。始制有名，名亦既有，夫亦将知止，知止可以不殆。譬道之在天下，犹川谷之于江海。

Chapter 33 · 第三十三章

Knowing others is intelligence; knowing oneself is enlightenment. Overcoming others is strength; overcoming oneself is power. Contentment is wealth; perseverance is purpose. Staying in one's place brings endurance; dying without perishing is true longevity.

知人者智，自知者明。胜人者有力，自胜者强。知足者富。强行者有志。不失其所者久。死而不亡者寿。

Chapter 34 · 第三十四章

The great Way overflows; it can go left or right. All things depend on it for life, and it does not refuse them; it accomplishes without claiming. It clothes and feeds all things without ruling them; it may be called small. All things return to it without being ruled; it may be called great. Because it never makes itself great, it achieves greatness.

大道泛兮，其可左右。万物恃之以生而不辞，功成而不有。衣养万物而不为主，可名于小；万物归焉而不为主，可名为大。以其终不自为大，故能成其大。

Chapter 35 · 第三十五章

Hold to the great image, and the world will come; it comes unharmed, in peace and safety. Music and delicacies detain the passing guest; but the Way spoken aloud is plain and tasteless — looked at, it cannot be seen; listened to, it cannot be heard; used, it cannot be exhausted.

执大象，天下往。往而不害，安平泰。乐与饵，过客止。道之出口，淡乎其无味，视之不足见，听之不足闻，用之不足既。

Chapter 36 · 第三十六章

What is to be contracted must first be expanded; what is to be weakened must first be strengthened; what is to be discarded must first be exalted; what is to be taken must first be given. This is called subtle insight. The soft and weak overcome the hard and strong. Fish should not leave the deep; the instruments of the state should not be shown to others.

将欲歙之，必固张之；将欲弱之，必固强之；将欲废之，必固兴之；将欲取之，必固与之。是谓微明。柔弱胜刚强。鱼不可脱于渊，国之利器不可以示人。

Chapter 37 · 第三十七章

The Way constantly acts without action, and nothing is left undone. If rulers could keep it, all things would transform of themselves. When transformation stirs desire, I would subdue it with nameless simplicity. Nameless simplicity is free of desire; free of desire and still, the world settles of itself.

道常无为而无不为。侯王若能守之，万物将自化。化而欲作，吾将镇之以无名之朴。无名之朴，夫亦将无欲。不欲以静，天下将自定。

Chapter 38 · 第三十八章

Higher virtue is not virtuous; therefore it has virtue. Lower virtue never neglects virtue; therefore it has no virtue. Higher virtue acts without intention; lower virtue acts with intention. Higher benevolence acts without intention; higher righteousness acts with intention. Higher rites act, and when none respond, sleeves are rolled and force applied. Thus, after the Way is lost comes virtue; after virtue, benevolence; after benevolence, righteousness; after righteousness, rites. Rites are the thinning of loyalty and trust, and the beginning of disorder. Foreknowledge is the flower of the Way, and the beginning of folly. Therefore the great man dwells in the thick, not the thin; in the substance, not the flower. He rejects the latter and chooses the former.

上德不德，是以有德；下德不失德，是以无德。上德无为而无以为；下德无为而有以为。上仁为之而无以为；上义为之而有以为。上礼为之而莫之应，则攘臂而扔之。故失道而后德，失德而后仁，失仁而后义，失义而后礼。夫礼者，忠信之薄，而乱之首。前识者，道之华，而愚之始。是以大丈夫处其厚，不居其薄；处其实，不居其华。故去彼取此。

Chapter 39 · 第三十九章

Those of old who attained the One: heaven attained it and became clear; earth and became tranquil; spirits and became efficacious; valleys and became full; all things and came to life; kings and the world was ordered. Without clarity, heaven would split; without tranquility, earth would quake; without efficacy, spirits would fade; without fullness, valleys would dry; without life, things would perish; without order, kings would fall. Thus honor takes lowliness as its root; height takes low ground as its foundation. Therefore kings call themselves orphaned, widowed, and unworthy — is this not taking lowliness as root? Is it not so? Hence the highest praise needs no praise. Do not aspire to the luster of jade, but to the hardness of stone.

昔之得一者：天得一以清；地得一以宁；神得一以灵；谷得一以盈；万物得一以生；侯王得一以为天下正。其致之也，天无以清，将恐裂；地无以宁，将恐废；神无以灵，将恐歇；谷无以盈，将恐竭；万物无以生，将恐灭；侯王无以正，将恐蹶。故贵以贱为本，高以下为基。是以侯王自称孤寡不谷。此非以贱为本邪？非乎？故至誉无誉。是故不欲琭琭如玉，珞珞如石。

Chapter 40 · 第四十章

Reversal is the motion of the Way; weakness is the function of the Way. All things under heaven are born of being; being is born of non-being.

反者道之动；弱者道之用。天下万物生于有，有生于无。

Chapter 41 · 第四十一章

When the highest man hears of the Way, he diligently practices it; the middling man hears and half remembers; the lowest hears and laughs aloud. Without laughter, it would not be the Way. Thus the old sayings: the bright Way seems dim; progress seems retreat; the smooth Way seems rough; higher virtue seems like a valley; great purity seems stained; broad virtue seems insufficient; steadfast virtue seems lax; true substance seems changeable; the great square has no corners; the great vessel is slow to finish; the great sound is scarcely heard; the great image has no shape — the Way is hidden and nameless. Yet the Way alone aids and completes all things.

上士闻道，勤而行之；中士闻道，若存若亡；下士闻道，大笑之。不笑不足以为道。故建言有之：明道若昧；进道若退；夷道若颣；上德若谷；大白若辱；广德若不足；建德若偷；质真若渝；大方无隅；大器晚成；大音希声；大象无形；道隐无名。夫唯道，善贷且成。

Chapter 42 · 第四十二章

The Way gives birth to One; One gives birth to Two; Two gives birth to Three; Three gives birth to all things. All things carry yin and embrace yang, blending their energies into harmony. What people dislike — being orphaned, widowed, unworthy — is what kings call themselves. Things may be diminished and so increased, or increased and so diminished. What others teach, I also teach: the violent and forceful do not die a natural death. I take this as the father of my teaching.

道生一，一生二，二生三，三生万物。万物负阴而抱阳，冲气以为和。人之所恶，唯孤寡不谷，而王公以为称。故物或损之而益，或益之而损。人之所教，我亦教之。强梁者不得其死，吾将以为教父。

Chapter 43 · 第四十三章

The softest thing in the world rides through the hardest. Non-being enters where there is no space. Thus I know the benefit of non-action. Wordless teaching and the benefit of non-action — few in the world attain them.

天下之至柔，驰骋天下之至坚。无有入无间，吾是以知无为之有益。不言之教，无为之益，天下希及之。

Chapter 44 · 第四十四章

Name or body, which is dearer? Body or wealth, which matters more? Gain or loss, which is the greater ill? Great love costs greatly; hoarding invites heavy loss. Knowing contentment avoids disgrace; knowing when to stop avoids peril; thus one endures.

名与身孰亲？身与货孰多？得与亡孰病？甚爱必大费；多藏必厚亡。故知足不辱，知止不殆，可以长久。

Chapter 45 · 第四十五章

Great perfection seems flawed, yet its use never wears out. Great fullness seems empty, yet its use never runs dry. Great straightness seems bent; great skill seems clumsy; great eloquence seems hesitant. Stillness overcomes heat; calm overcomes haste. Perfect clarity is the order of the world.

大成若缺，其用不弊。大盈若冲，其用不穷。大直若屈，大巧若拙，大辩若讷。静胜躁，寒胜热。清静为天下正。

Chapter 46 · 第四十六章

When the world has the Way, horses are used for farming; when the world lacks the Way, war horses breed in the suburbs. No calamity is greater than discontent; no fault is greater than wanting more. Therefore, contentment with contentment is lasting contentment.

天下有道，却走马以粪。天下无道，戎马生于郊。祸莫大于不知足；咎莫大于欲得。故知足之足，常足矣。

Chapter 47 · 第四十七章

Without leaving the door, one knows the world; without looking out the window, one sees the Way of heaven. The farther one goes, the less one knows. Therefore the sage knows without traveling, names without seeing, accomplishes without acting.

不出户，知天下；不窥牖，见天道。其出弥远，其知弥少。是以圣人不行而知，不见而明，不为而成。

Chapter 48 · 第四十八章

In learning, one increases daily; in the Way, one decreases daily. Decrease and decrease again, until one reaches non-action. Through non-action, nothing is left undone. To win the world, act without design; if one has designs, one is not worthy of the world.

为学日益，为道日损。损之又损，以至于无为。无为而无不为。取天下常以无事，及其有事，不足以取天下。

Chapter 49 · 第四十九章

The sage has no fixed heart; the people's heart is his heart. To the good I am good; to the not-good I am also good — thus goodness is attained. To the trusting I am true; to the not-trusting I am also true — thus trust is attained. In the world the sage is reserved, treating all hearts as children's.

圣人常无心，以百姓心为心。善者，吾善之；不善者，吾亦善之；德善。信者，吾信之；不信者，吾亦信之；德信。圣人在天下，歙歙焉，为天下浑其心，百姓皆注其耳目，圣人皆孩之。

Chapter 50 · 第五十章

Life comes out and death enters. Of the living, three in ten are on the path of life; three in ten on the path of death; and three in ten who move toward death through clinging to life. Why? Because they cling too hard to life. I have heard that one who masters life walks without meeting rhinos or tigers, enters battle without armor or blade — the rhino finds no place for its horn, the tiger no place for its claw, the soldier no place for his blade. Why? Because he has no place for death.

出生入死。生之徒，十有三；死之徒，十有三；人之生，动之于死地，亦十有三。夫何故？以其生生之厚。盖闻善摄生者，陆行不遇兕虎，入军不被甲兵；兕无所投其角，虎无所用其爪，兵无所容其刃。夫何故？以其无死地。

Chapter 51 · 第五十一章

The Way gives them life; virtue rears them; matter shapes them; conditions complete them. Thus all things honor the Way and esteem virtue. This honor is not commanded but constant and natural. So the Way gives life, virtue rears, grows, nurtures, matures, completes, nourishes, and shelters. It gives life without possessing; acts without claiming; raises without dominating. This is the profound virtue.

道生之，德畜之，物形之，势成之。是以万物莫不尊道而贵德。道之尊，德之贵，夫莫之命而常自然。故道生之，德畜之；长之育之；成之熟之；养之覆之。生而不有，为而不恃，长而不宰，是谓玄德。

Chapter 52 · 第五十二章

The world has a beginning, which is the mother of the world. Having found the mother, one knows her children; knowing the children, one returns to keeping the mother, and to the end of life one is free of danger. Block the passages and close the doors, and one is never exhausted; open the passages and meddle in affairs, and one is beyond saving. To see the small is enlightenment; to keep the soft is strength. Use the light, return to brightness, leave no harm to the body — this is practicing the constant.

天下有始，以为天下母。既得其母，以知其子；既知其子，复守其母，没身不殆。塞其兑，闭其门，终身不勤。开其兑，济其事，终身不救。见小曰明，守柔曰强。用其光，复归其明，无遗身殃；是为袭常。

Chapter 53 · 第五十三章

If I had the least knowledge, walking on the great Way, I would fear only the bypaths. The great Way is smooth, yet people love the side paths. The court is corrupt, the fields overgrown, the granaries empty, yet they wear fine robes, carry sharp swords, gorge on food, and hoard wealth. This is robbery and extravagance — not the Way at all!

使我介然有知，行于大道，唯施是畏。大道甚夷，而人好径。朝甚除，田甚芜，仓甚虚；服文彩，带利剑，厌饮食，财货有余；是谓盗夸。非道也哉！

Chapter 54 · 第五十四章

What is well built cannot be uprooted; what is well embraced cannot slip away; descendants will honor it without end. Cultivate it in the self, and virtue is real; in the family, it overflows; in the village, it grows; in the state, it abounds; in the world, it is universal. Thus see the self in the self, the family in the family, the village in the village, the state in the state, the world in the world. How do I know the world is so? By this.

善建者不拔，善抱者不脱，子孙以祭祀不辍。修之于身，其德乃真；修之于家，其德乃余；修之于乡，其德乃长；修之于邦，其德乃丰；修之于天下，其德乃普。故以身观身，以家观家，以乡观乡，以邦观邦，以天下观天下。吾何以知天下然哉？以此。

Chapter 55 · 第五十五章

One rich in virtue is like a newborn child: poisonous insects do not sting, wild beasts do not seize, birds of prey do not strike. Bones soft and tendons weak, yet the grip is firm; unaware of the union of male and female, yet full of vigor — the perfection of essence. Crying all day without growing hoarse — the perfection of harmony. Knowing harmony is the constant; knowing the constant is enlightenment; forcing life is ill fortune; letting breath serve the heart is force. Things that grow strong grow old — this is not the Way; what is not the Way ends early.

含德之厚，比于赤子。毒虫不螫，猛兽不据，攫鸟不搏。骨弱筋柔而握固。未知牝牡之合而全作，精之至也。终日号而不嗄，和之至也。知和曰常，知常曰明，益生曰祥。心使气曰强。物壮则老，谓之不道，不道早已。

Chapter 56 · 第五十六章

Those who know do not speak; those who speak do not know. Block the passages, close the doors, blunt the sharp, untangle the knots, soften the glare, merge with the dust — this is called mysterious union. Thus one cannot be made intimate, nor estranged; cannot be benefited, nor harmed; cannot be exalted, nor debased. Therefore one is esteemed by all under heaven.

知者不言，言者不知。塞其兑，闭其门，挫其锐，解其分，和其光，同其尘，是谓玄同。故不可得而亲，不可得而疏；不可得而利，不可得而害；不可得而贵，不可得而贱。故为天下贵。

Chapter 57 · 第五十七章

Govern the state with rectitude; use war with surprise; win the world with non-action. How do I know? Thus: the more taboos the world has, the poorer the people; the more weapons, the darker the state; the more cleverness, the stranger the things; the more laws, the more thieves. Therefore the sage says: I act without action, and the people transform themselves; I love stillness, and the people correct themselves; I do not meddle, and the people enrich themselves; I have no desires, and the people return to simplicity.

以正治国，以奇用兵，以无事取天下。吾何以知其然哉？以此：天下多忌讳，而民弥贫；民多利器，国家滋昏；人多伎巧，奇物滋起；法令滋彰，盗贼多有。故圣人云：我无为，而民自化；我好静，而民自正；我无事，而民自富；我无欲，而民自朴。

Chapter 58 · 第五十八章

When government is lenient, the people are simple; when it is strict, the people are cunning. Misfortune is the shadow of fortune; fortune is the omen of misfortune. Who knows their end? There is no fixed rule: the upright reverts to the strange, goodness reverts to the monstrous. Long has the people been deluded. Therefore the sage is square without cutting, angular without piercing, straight without being harsh, bright without dazzling.

其政闷闷，其民淳淳；其政察察，其民缺缺。祸兮福之所倚，福兮祸之所伏。孰知其极？其无正也。正复为奇，善复为妖。人之迷，其日固久。是以圣人方而不割，廉而不刿，直而不肆，光而不耀。

Chapter 59 · 第五十九章

In governing people and serving heaven, nothing is better than frugality. Frugality means early submission; early submission means accumulating virtue; with accumulated virtue, nothing is impossible; with nothing impossible, none knows one's limit; when none knows one's limit, one may hold the state; holding the state's root, one endures. This is the Way of deep roots and firm stalks — lasting life and lasting sight.

治人事天，莫若啬。夫唯啬，是谓早服；早服谓之重积德；重积德则无不克；无不克则莫知其极；莫知其极，可以有国；有国之母，可以长久；是谓深根固柢，长生久视之道。

Chapter 60 · 第六十章

Governing a great state is like cooking a small fish. Rule the world with the Way, and its ghosts lose power; not only the ghosts, but the spirits do not harm the people; not only do the spirits not harm the people, the sage does not harm them either. When neither harms the other, virtue flows back to both.

治大国，若烹小鲜。以道莅天下，其鬼不神；非其鬼不神，其神不伤人；非其神不伤人，圣人亦不伤人。夫两不相伤，故德交归焉。

Chapter 61 · 第六十一章

A great state flows low; it is the confluence of the world, the female of the world. The female often overcomes the male through stillness, taking the lower place. Thus a great state that lowers itself before smaller states wins them; a small state that lowers itself before greater states is won. So some lower themselves to win, others lower themselves and are won. Great states wish only to nurture others; small states wish only to serve. Both get their wish, but the great must take the lower place.

大国者下流，天下之交，天下之牝。牝常以静胜牡，以静为下。故大国以下小国，则取小国；小国以下大国，则取大国。故或下以取，或下而取。大国不过欲兼畜人，小国不过欲入事人。夫两者各得所欲，大者宜为下。

Chapter 62 · 第六十二章

The Way is the refuge of all things — the treasure of the good, the protection of the not-good. Fine words can win honor; fine conduct can win esteem. If a man is not good, why abandon him? Therefore, in crowning the emperor and appointing ministers, though one bears jade before teams of horses, it is better to sit and offer this Way. The ancients valued it because, seeking, one finds, and, guilty, one is freed. Thus it is the most precious thing under heaven.

道者万物之奥。善人之宝，不善人之所保。美言可以市尊，美行可以加人。人之不善，何弃之有？故立天子，置三公，虽有拱璧以先驷马，不如坐进此道。古之所以贵此道者何？不曰：求以得，有罪以免邪？故为天下贵。

Chapter 63 · 第六十三章

Act without action; work without effort; taste without tasting. Great or small, many or few, repay resentment with virtue. Plan for the difficult while it is easy; do the great while it is small. All difficult things begin easy; all great things begin small. Thus the sage never strives for greatness, and so achieves greatness. Light promises invite little trust; many easy things invite many difficulties. Thus the sage treats everything as difficult, and so meets no difficulty.

为无为，事无事，味无味。大小多少，报怨以德。图难于其易，为大于其细；天下难事，必作于易；天下大事，必作于细。是以圣人终不为大，故能成其大。夫轻诺必寡信，多易必多难。是以圣人犹难之，故终无难矣。

Chapter 64 · 第六十四章

What is at rest is easy to hold; what has not yet appeared is easy to plan for; what is brittle is easy to break; what is small is easy to scatter. Act before it exists; order before disorder arises. A tree a fathom thick grows from a hair; a tower of nine stories rises from a mound of earth; a journey of a thousand miles begins with a single step. Those who act ruin; those who grasp lose. Thus the sage acts without acting and so ruins nothing, grasps without grasping and so loses nothing. People often fail just before success; be as careful at the end as at the beginning, and nothing fails. Therefore the sage desires not to desire, does not prize rare goods, learns not to learn, and returns to where the many have gone astray, aiding the natural way of all things without daring to act.

其安易持，其未兆易谋；其脆易泮，其微易散。为之于未有，治之于未乱。合抱之木，生于毫末；九层之台，起于累土；千里之行，始于足下。为者败之，执者失之。是以圣人无为故无败，无执故无失。民之从事，常于几成而败之。慎终如始，则无败事。是以圣人欲不欲，不贵难得之货；学不学，复众人之所过，以辅万物之自然，而不敢为。

Chapter 65 · 第六十五章

The ancient masters of the Way did not enlighten the people with it, but kept them simple. The people are hard to govern because they are too clever. To rule by cleverness is the nation's curse; to rule without it is the nation's blessing. Knowing both is a pattern; constantly keeping the pattern is the mysterious virtue. Profound and far-reaching, it turns back with all things, and so reaches the great harmony.

古之善为道者，非以明民，将以愚之。民之难治，以其智多。故以智治国，国之贼；不以智治国，国之福。知此两者亦稽式。常知稽式，是谓玄德。玄德深矣，远矣，与物反矣，然后乃至大顺。

Chapter 66 · 第六十六章

Rivers and seas become kings of all valleys because they lie below them. Thus the sage who would be above the people must speak humbly; who would lead them must place himself behind. Thus he dwells above, yet the people are not burdened; leads in front, yet they are not harmed. The world delights in supporting him without wearying. Because he does not contend, none can contend with him.

江海之所以能为百谷王者，以其善下之，故能为百谷王。是以圣人欲上民，必以言下之；欲先民，必以身后之。是以圣人处上而民不重，处前而民不害。是以天下乐推而不厌。以其不争，故天下莫能与之争。

Chapter 67 · 第六十七章

All under heaven say my Way is great but seems unlike anything. Precisely because it is great, it seems unlike; if it were like, long since it would be small. I have three treasures; hold and keep them: the first is compassion, the second frugality, the third not daring to be first in the world. Compassion gives courage; frugality gives breadth; not daring to be first makes one the vessel of leadership. Today, abandoning compassion for courage, frugality for breadth, the rear for the front — this is death. Compassion in battle brings victory, in defense security; heaven will save what compassion guards.

天下皆谓我道大，似不肖。夫唯大，故似不肖。若肖，久矣其细也夫！我有三宝，持而保之：一曰慈，二曰俭，三曰不敢为天下先。慈故能勇；俭故能广；不敢为天下先，故能成器长。今舍慈且勇；舍俭且广；舍后且先；死矣！夫慈，以战则胜，以守则固。天将救之，以慈卫之。

Chapter 68 · 第六十八章

The good commander is not warlike; the good warrior is not angry; the good victor does not contend; the good leader of men is humble. This is the virtue of non-contention, the power of using others, the ultimate of heaven since ancient times.

善为士者，不武；善战者，不怒；善胜敌者，不与；善用人者，为之下。是谓不争之德，是谓用人之力，是谓配天古之极。

Chapter 69 · 第六十九章

In warfare there is a saying: I dare not be the host, but the guest; I dare not advance an inch, but retreat a foot. This is to march without marching, to roll up sleeves without arms, to confront without enemies, to wield weapons without blades. No greater calamity than underestimating the enemy; to underestimate the enemy nearly loses my treasures. Thus when equal forces meet, the mourning side wins.

用兵有言：吾不敢为主，而为客；不敢进寸，而退尺。是谓行无行；攘无臂；扔无敌；执无兵。祸莫大于轻敌，轻敌几丧吾宝。故抗兵相若，哀者胜矣。

Chapter 70 · 第七十章

My words are easy to understand, easy to practice, yet none under heaven understand or practice them. Words have their source; affairs have their master. Because they do not know, they do not know me. Few know me; therefore I am precious. Thus the sage wears coarse cloth but holds jade in his heart.

吾言甚易知，甚易行。天下莫能知，莫能行。言有宗，事有君。夫唯无知，是以不我知。知我者希，则我者贵。是以圣人被褐而怀玉。

Chapter 71 · 第七十一章

To know that one does not know is best; not to know yet claim to know is sickness. The sage is not sick because he is sick of this sickness. Being sick of sickness, he is free of it.

知不知，尚矣；不知知，病也。圣人不病，以其病病。夫唯病病，是以不病。

Chapter 72 · 第七十二章

When the people do not fear authority, greater authority comes. Do not confine their dwellings; do not oppress their lives. Because he does not oppress, he is not oppressed. Thus the sage knows himself without displaying, loves himself without exalting; he rejects the one and takes the other.

民不畏威，则大威至。无狎其所居，无厌其所生。夫唯不厌，是以不厌。是以圣人自知不自见；自爱不自贵。故去彼取此。

Chapter 73 · 第七十三章

Courage in daring kills; courage in not daring lives. Of the two, one benefits, one harms. What heaven hates, who knows the reason? Thus the sage treats it as difficult. The Way of heaven contends without fighting, responds without speaking, summons without calling, plans unhurriedly. Heaven's net is vast; its mesh is wide, yet nothing slips through.

勇于敢则杀，勇于不敢则活。此两者，或利或害。天之所恶，孰知其故？是以圣人犹难之。天之道，不争而善胜，不言而善应，不召而自来，繟然而善谋。天网恢恢，疏而不失。

Chapter 74 · 第七十四章

If the people do not fear death, how can death frighten them? If they always feared death, and the wicked were seized and killed, who would dare? There is always a lord of execution. To kill in his place is like cutting wood in the master carpenter's place; few who cut in the carpenter's place do not injure their hands.

民不畏死，奈何以死惧之？若使民常畏死，而为奇者，吾得执而杀之，孰敢？常有司杀者杀。夫代司杀者杀，是谓代大匠斫。夫代大匠斫者，希有不伤其手矣。

Chapter 75 · 第七十五章

The people starve because their rulers levy heavy taxes — that is why they starve. The people are hard to govern because their rulers interfere — that is why they are hard to govern. The people risk death because their rulers cling to life — that is why they risk it. One who does not cling to life is worthier than one who prizes living.

民之饥，以其上食税之多，是以饥。民之难治，以其上之有为，是以难治。民之轻死，以其上求生之厚，是以轻死。夫唯无以生为者，是贤于贵生。

Chapter 76 · 第七十六章

In life, man is soft and supple; in death, rigid. All things — grass and trees — are soft and tender in life, withered and dry in death. Thus the hard and strong belong to death; the soft and weak belong to life. Therefore strong weapons do not win; strong trees are felled. The strong and great take the lower place; the soft and weak the higher.

人之生也柔弱，其死也坚强。万物草木之生也柔脆，其死也枯槁。故坚强者死之徒，柔弱者生之徒。是以兵强则灭，木强则折。强大处下，柔弱处上。

Chapter 77 · 第七十七章

The Way of heaven — is it not like a drawn bow? What is high it presses down; what is low it lifts; what has excess it diminishes; what lacks it supplies. The Way of heaven diminishes the excessive and supplements the lacking. The way of man is otherwise: it takes from the lacking to serve the excessive. Who can use excess to serve the world? Only one with the Way. Thus the sage acts without claiming, succeeds without dwelling; he does not wish to display his worth.

天之道，其犹张弓与？高者抑之，下者举之；有余者损之，不足者补之。天之道，损有余而补不足。人之道，则不然，损不足以奉有余。孰能有余以奉天下？唯有道者。是以圣人为而不恃，功成而不处，其不欲见贤。

Chapter 78 · 第七十八章

Nothing under heaven is softer than water, yet nothing conquers the hard as water does; nothing can change it. The weak overcome the strong, the soft overcome the hard — none under heaven know this, none can practice it. Thus the sage says: to bear the state's filth is to be lord of the land; to bear the state's misfortune is to be king of the world. Straight words sound paradoxical.

天下莫柔弱于水，而攻坚强者莫之能胜，其无以易之。弱之胜强，柔之胜刚，天下莫不知，莫能行。是以圣人云：受国之垢，是谓社稷主；受国不祥，是谓天下王。正言若反。

Chapter 79 · 第七十九章

To settle a great grievance, some resentment always remains; how can this be called good? Thus the sage holds the left half of the contract and presses no one. The virtuous keep the contract; the virtuousless pursue collection. The Way of heaven has no favorites; it is always with the good person.

和大怨，必有余怨，安可以为善？是以圣人执左契，而不责于人。有德司契，无德司彻。天道无亲，常与善人。

Chapter 80 · 第八十章

Let the state be small with few people. Though tools exist ten- and hundredfold, they are unused; the people value their lives and do not migrate far. Boats and carriages exist, but none ride them; armor and weapons exist, but none display them. Return to knotted cords in place of writing. Relish your food, admire your clothes, rest in your homes, delight in your customs. Neighboring states see each other, hear each other's chickens and dogs, yet the people grow old and die without visiting one another.

小国寡民。使有什伯之器而不用；使民重死而不远徙。虽有舟舆，无所乘之；虽有甲兵，无所陈之。使民复结绳而用之。甘其食，美其服，安其居，乐其俗。邻国相望，鸡犬之声相闻，民至老死，不相往来。

Chapter 81 · 第八十一章

True words are not beautiful; beautiful words are not true. The good do not argue; those who argue are not good. The wise are not learned; the learned are not wise. The sage does not hoard; the more he does for others, the more he has; the more he gives, the richer he is. The Way of heaven benefits without harming; the Way of the sage acts without contending.

信言不美，美言不信。善者不辩，辩者不善。知者不博，博者不知。圣人不积，既以为人己愈有，既以与人己愈多。天之道，利而不害；圣人之道，为而不争。

Tao Te Ching (Mawangdui Silk Manuscript Version A)

The Mawangdui Silk Manuscript Version A (马王堆帛书《老子》甲本) in the identical bilingual layout as the standard received version — English on the left, Chinese on the right — with an editable text option for readers.

By Laozi
Chapter 1 · 第一章

The way that can be spoken of is not the eternal Way; the name that can be named is not the eternal Name. The nameless is the beginning of all things; the named is the mother of all things. Therefore, constantly free of desire, one observes its mysteries; constantly filled with desire, one observes its boundaries. These two come from the same source but differ in name; together they are called the mystery. The mystery of mysteries is the gateway to all wonders.

道，可道也，非恒道也。名，可名也，非恒名也。无名，万物之始也；有名，万物之母也。故恒无欲也，以观其眇；恒有欲也，以观其所徼。两者同出，异名同胃，玄之又玄，众眇之门。

Chapter 2 · 第二章

When all under heaven know beauty as beauty, ugliness arises; when all know good as good, evil arises. Thus being and non-being produce each other; difficult and easy complete each other; long and short contrast each other; high and low incline toward each other; sound and voice harmonize; before and after follow. Therefore the sage acts without action and teaches without words. All things arise and he does not forsake them; he gives them life without possessing; he acts without relying; he succeeds without dwelling. Precisely because he does not dwell on it, his merit never leaves him.

天下皆知美之为美，恶已；皆知善，斯不善矣。有无之相生也，难易之相成也，长短之相形也，高下之相盈也，音声之相和也，先后之相随也。是以圣人居无为之事，行不言之教。万物作而弗始也，为而弗恃也，成功而弗居也。夫唯弗居，是以不去。

Chapter 3 · 第三章

Do not exalt the worthy, and the people will not compete. Do not prize rare goods, and the people will not steal. Do not display what is desirable, and the people's hearts will not be disturbed. Thus the sage governs by emptying their hearts, filling their bellies, weakening their ambitions, strengthening their bones. Always keep the people free of knowledge and desire, so that the clever dare not act. Act without action, and nothing is left ungoverned.

不上贤，使民不争。不贵难得之货，使民不为盗。不见可欲，使民不乱。是以圣人之治也，虚其心，实其腹，弱其志，强其骨。恒使民无知无欲也，使夫知不敢弗为而已，则无不治矣。

Chapter 4 · 第四章

The Way is empty, yet when used it is never exhausted. Bottomless, it seems the ancestor of all things. It blunts the sharp, unties the tangled, softens the glare, merges with the dust. Hidden, it seems barely present. I do not know whose child it is; it appears to precede the Lord of Heaven.

道冲，而用之有弗盈也。渊呵，似万物之宗。挫其锐，解其纷，和其光，同其尘。湛呵，似或存。吾不知谁之子也，象帝之先。

Chapter 5 · 第五章

Heaven and earth are not benevolent; they treat all things as straw dogs. The sage is not benevolent; he treats the people as straw dogs. Between heaven and earth, is it not like a bellows? Empty yet inexhaustible, the more it moves, the more it produces. Many words lead to exhaustion; better to hold to the center.

天地不仁，以万物为刍狗。圣人不仁，以百姓为刍狗。天地之间，其犹橐籥乎？虚而不屈，动而愈出。多闻数穷，不若守于中。

Chapter 6 · 第六章

The valley spirit never dies; it is called the mysterious feminine. The gate of the mysterious feminine is the root of heaven and earth. Continuous and unbroken, its use is never worn out.

谷神不死，是谓玄牝。玄牝之门，是谓天地之根。绵绵呵若存，用之不勤。

Chapter 7 · 第七章

Heaven is enduring and earth is lasting. Heaven and earth can endure because they do not live for themselves; thus they can live long. Therefore the sage places himself behind, yet comes first; treats himself as outside, yet endures. Is it not because he has no self-interest that his self-interest is fulfilled?

天长地久。天地所以能长且久者，以其不自生也，故能长生。是以圣人退其身而身先，外其身而身存。不以其无私与？故能成其私。

Chapter 8 · 第八章

The highest good is like water. Water benefits all things without contending, and settles where others disdain; thus it is close to the Way. In dwelling, choose the ground; in mind, choose depth; in association, choose benevolence; in words, choose trust; in government, choose order; in action, choose ability; in timing, choose the moment. Because he does not contend, he is beyond reproach.

上善若水。水善利万物而有静，居众人之所恶，故几于道矣。居善地，心善渊，予善信，正善治，事善能，动善时。夫唯不争，故无尤。

Chapter 9 · 第九章

Rather than fill a vessel to overflowing, stop in time. Sharpen an edge too much, and it cannot be kept long. A hall filled with gold and jade cannot be guarded. Wealth and honor with arrogance invite disaster. Withdraw when the work is done — this is the Way of heaven.

持而盈之，不若其已。揣而锐之，不可长保也。金玉盈室，莫之能守也。富贵而骄，自遗其咎。功遂身退，天之道也。

Chapter 10 · 第十章

Can you carry body and soul together without separation? Can you concentrate your breath to become as soft as a newborn child? Can you cleanse your inner mirror so it is without blemish? Can you love the people and govern the land without action? Can you open and close the gates of heaven like the feminine? Can you understand all things without knowledge? It gives life and nurtures; gives life without possessing; acts without claiming; raises without dominating. This is the profound virtue.

载营魄抱一，能无离乎？抟气致柔，能婴儿乎？涤除玄鉴，能无疵乎？爱民治国，能无知乎？天门启阖，能为雌乎？明白四达，能无为乎？生之畜之，生而弗有，长而弗宰，是谓玄德。

Chapter 11 · 第十一章

Thirty spokes share one hub; in its emptiness lies the usefulness of the wheel. Clay is shaped into a vessel; in its emptiness lies the usefulness of the vessel. Doors and windows are cut to make a room; in their emptiness lies the usefulness of the room. Therefore, being provides advantage, but non-being provides usefulness.

三十辐同一毂，当其无，有车之用也。埏埴以为器，当其无，有器之用也。凿户牖以为室，当其无，有室之用也。故有之以为利，无之以为用。

Chapter 12 · 第十二章

The five colors blind the eye; the five tones deafen the ear; the five flavors dull the palate; racing and hunting madden the mind; rare goods lead astray. Therefore the sage is for the belly, not for the eye; he rejects the one and chooses the other.

五色令人目盲；五音令人耳聋；五味令人口爽；驰骋畋猎令人心发狂；难得之货令人行妨。是以圣人为腹不为目，故去彼取此。

Chapter 13 · 第十三章

Favor and disgrace are alike a cause for alarm; value great troubles as you value your body. What does it mean that favor and disgrace are alike a cause for alarm? Favor lifts one up; gaining it is alarming, losing it is alarming. What does it mean that great troubles are valued as the body? If I had no body, what trouble could I have? Therefore, one who values the world as his body may be entrusted with the world; one who loves the world as himself may be given the world.

宠辱若惊，贵大患若身。何谓宠辱若惊？宠之为下也，得之若惊，失之若惊，是谓宠辱若惊。何谓贵大患若身？吾所以有大患者，为吾有身，及吾无身，有何患？故贵为身于为天下，若可以托天下矣；爱以身为天下，若可以寄天下矣。

Chapter 14 · 第十四章

Looked at but unseen, it is called the invisible; listened to but unheard, it is called the inaudible; grasped but untouched, it is called the subtle. These three cannot be fully grasped, so they merge into one. Its rising is not bright, its setting is not dark; endless and unnameable, it returns to nothingness. This is the formless form, the imageless image, called the elusive. Meet it and you see no face; follow it and you see no back. Hold to the ancient Way to master the present; knowing the ancient beginning is the thread of the Way.

视之而弗见，名之曰微；听之而弗闻，名之曰希；捪之而弗得，名之曰夷。三者不可致诘，故混而为一。一者，其上不攸，其下不忽。寻寻呵不可名也，复归于无物。是谓无状之状，无物之象，是谓忽恍。随而不见其后，迎而不见其首。执今之道，以御今之有。以知古始，是谓道纪。

Chapter 15 · 第十五章

The ancient masters were subtle, mysterious, and profound beyond understanding. Because they cannot be understood, one can only describe them: cautious, as if crossing a frozen stream; hesitant, as if fearing neighbors; reserved, as if guests; yielding, as melting ice; simple, as uncarved wood; open, as a valley; murky, as muddy water. Who can remain muddy until it slowly clears? Who can remain still until life slowly stirs? Those who keep this Way do not seek fullness; not being full, they can be renewed and endure.

古之善为士者，必微妙玄达，深不可识，是以为之容：豫乎若冬涉川，犹乎其若畏四邻，严乎其若客，涣乎其若释，敦乎其若朴，混乎其若浊。孰能浊以静之徐清？孰能安以动之徐生？保此道者不欲盈。夫唯不盈，是以能蔽而不成。

Chapter 16 · 第十六章

Reach the utmost emptiness and guard perfect stillness. All things arise together; I watch them return. Each thing flourishes and returns to its root. Returning to the root is stillness; this is called fulfilling life. Fulfilling life is the constant; knowing the constant is enlightenment. Not knowing the constant, one acts recklessly and invites misfortune. Knowing the constant brings tolerance; tolerance brings impartiality; impartiality brings kingship; kingship draws near heaven; heaven draws near the Way; abiding in the Way, one endures to the end of life without peril.

至虚极也，守情表也。万物旁作，吾以观其复也。天物云云，各复归于其根。归根曰情，是谓复命。复命曰常，知常曰明。不知常，妄作凶。知常容，容乃公，公乃王，王乃天，天乃道，道乃久，没身不殆。

Chapter 17 · 第十七章

The best ruler is barely known to exist; the next is loved and praised; the next is feared; the worst is despised. When trust is insufficient, distrust follows. The sage values few words; when work is done and affairs succeed, the people all say, "It happened naturally."

大上，下知有之；其次，亲誉之；其次，畏之；其下，侮之。信不足，安有不信。犹呵，其贵言也。成功遂事，而百姓谓我自然。

Chapter 18 · 第十八章

When the great Way is abandoned, benevolence and righteousness appear. When cleverness and wisdom emerge, great hypocrisy follows. When the six family relations fall into discord, filial piety and parental affection arise. When the state is in chaos, loyal ministers appear.

故大道废，安有仁义；知慧出，安有大伪；六亲不和，安有孝慈；邦家昏乱，安有忠臣。

Chapter 19 · 第十九章

Abandon sagehood and discard wisdom, and the people benefit a hundredfold. Abandon benevolence and discard righteousness, and the people return to filial piety. Abandon craft and discard profit, and robbers disappear. These three are adornments and not enough; therefore let there be something to rely on: embrace simplicity, reduce selfishness, lessen desires, abandon learning, and be free of worry.

绝圣弃知，民利百倍；绝仁弃义，民复孝慈；绝巧弃利，盗贼无有。此三言也，以为文未足，故令有所属：见素抱朴，少私寡欲，绝学无忧。

Chapter 20 · 第二十章

Between "yes" and "well, of course," how much difference is there? Between good and evil, how much? What others fear, one cannot but fear — how endless it all is! Most people are joyful, as if feasting at a great sacrifice or climbing a tower in spring. I alone am still, without sign, like an infant who has not yet smiled; listless, as if with no home. Others have more than enough; I alone seem to lack. Mine is the mind of a fool — so plain! Others are bright; I alone am dim. Others are sharp; I alone am dull, like the tranquil sea, drifting with nowhere to stop. Others have purpose; I alone am stubborn and lowly. I alone differ from others, and value the nourishing mother.

唯与诃，其相去几何？美与恶，其相去何若？人之所畏，亦不可以不畏人。惚呵，其未央哉！众人熙熙，若乡于大牢，而春登台。我泊焉未佻，若婴儿之未咳。纍呵，似无所归。众人皆有余，我独遗。我愚人之心也，惷惷呵。俗人昭昭，我独若昏；俗人察察，我独闷闷。忽呵其若海，望呵其若无所止。众人皆有以，我独门顽以鄙。我欲独异于人，而贵食母。

Chapter 21 · 第二十一章

The form of great virtue follows only the Way. The Way as a thing is elusive and vague. Vague and elusive, within it is an image; elusive and vague, within it is a substance; dim and dark, within it is essence. Its essence is most real; within it is faith. From ancient times to now, its name has not departed; through it one can survey the origin of all things. How do I know the state of all origins? By this.

孔德之容，唯道是从。道之物，唯望唯惚。惚呵望呵，中有象呵；望呵惚呵，中有物呵。幽呵冥呵，中有精呵；其精甚真，其中有信。自古及今，其名不去，以顺众父。吾何以知众父之然？以此。

Chapter 22 · 第二十二章

Yield and you remain whole; bend and you straighten; hollow and you fill; worn and you renew; have little and you gain; have much and you are lost. Therefore the sage embraces the One and becomes a model for the world. Not displaying himself, he shines; not asserting himself, he is distinguished; not claiming merit, he succeeds; not boasting, he endures. Because he does not contend, none in the world can contend with him. The ancient saying "yield and remain whole" — is it empty words? Truly, it brings one back to wholeness.

曲则全，枉则正，洼则盈，敝则新，少则得，多则惑。是以圣人抱一，以为天下式。不自见，故明；不自是，故彰；不自伐，故有功；不自矜，故长。夫唯不争，故天下莫能与之争。古之所谓曲则全者，几语才！诚全归之。

Chapter 23 · 第二十三章

To speak little is natural. A whirlwind does not last a morning; a downpour does not last a day. Who makes them? Heaven and earth. If heaven and earth cannot last long, how much less can man? Therefore, one who follows the Way is one with the Way; one who follows virtue is one with virtue; one who loses them is one with the loss. Where trust is lacking, there is no trust.

希言自然。飘风不终朝，暴雨不终日。孰为此？天地，而弗能久，又况于人乎？故从事而道者同于道，德者同于德，失者同于失。同于德者，道亦德之；同于失者，道亦失之。

Chapter 24 · 第二十四章

Those on tiptoe cannot stand; those with legs astride cannot walk. Those who see themselves are not illuminated; those who assert themselves are not distinguished; those who boast have no merit; those who are proud do not endure. In the Way, these are like leftover food and excess growth — all things detest them, so those who have the Way do not dwell on them.

企者不立，跨者不行。自见者不明，自是者不彰，自伐者无功，自矜者不长。其在道也，曰：余食赘行。物有恶之，故有道者不处也。

Chapter 25 · 第二十五章

There was a thing formed in chaos, born before heaven and earth. Silent and empty, standing alone and unchanging, circling without tiring — it can be the mother of heaven and earth. I do not know its name; I call it the Way, and force on it the name "great." Great means passing on; passing on means reaching far; reaching far means returning. Therefore the Way is great, heaven is great, earth is great, and man is also great. Within the realm there are four greats, and man is one of them. Man follows earth; earth follows heaven; heaven follows the Way; the Way follows what is natural.

有物混成，先天地生。寂呵寥呵，独立而不改，可以为天地母。吾未知其名，字之曰道，吾强为之名曰大。大曰逝，逝曰远，远曰反。道大，天大，地大，王亦大。国中有四大，而王居其一焉。人法地，地法天，天法道，道法自然。

Chapter 26 · 第二十六章

Heaviness is the root of lightness; stillness is the master of haste. Therefore the sage travels all day without leaving his baggage; though surrounded by splendor, he remains calm. How can the lord of ten thousand chariots treat his own body lightly? To be light is to lose the root; to be hasty is to lose the master.

重为轻根，静为躁君。是以君子终日行，不离其辎重。虽有环观，燕处则超然。奈何万乘之王，而以身轻于天下？轻则失本，躁则失君。

Chapter 27 · 第二十七章

Good traveling leaves no tracks; good words leave no flaws; good counting needs no tally; good closing needs no bolt, yet cannot be opened; good binding needs no rope, yet cannot be untied. Therefore the sage is always good at saving people, so no one is abandoned; always good at saving things, so nothing is wasted. This is called hidden illumination. So the good are teachers of the not-good, and the not-good are material for the good. Not valuing the teacher, not loving the material — though clever, one is greatly astray. This is the essential mystery.

善行者无辙迹，善言者无瑕谪，善数者不以筹策，善闭者无关钥而不可启也，善结者无绳约而不可解也。是以圣人恒善救人，而无弃人；物无弃材，是谓袭明。故善人，善人之师；不善人，善人之资也。不贵其师，不爱其资，虽知乎，大迷。是谓要妙。

Chapter 28 · 第二十八章

Know the masculine, keep to the feminine, and become the valley of the world. Being the valley of the world, constant virtue never departs; return to the innocence of a child. Know the white, keep to the black, and become the model of the world. Being the model, constant virtue does not err; return to the limitless. Know glory, keep to disgrace, and become the valley of the world. Being the valley, constant virtue is full; return to simplicity. When simplicity is dispersed, it becomes tools, and the sage uses them to govern. Thus the greatest carving cuts nothing.

知其雄，守其雌，为天下溪。为天下溪，恒德不离；恒德不离，复归于婴儿。知其白，守其辱，为天下谷。为天下谷，恒德乃足；恒德乃足，复归于朴。知其白，守其黑，为天下式。为天下式，恒德不忒；恒德不忒，复归于无极。朴散则为器，圣人用则为官长。夫大制无割。

Chapter 29 · 第二十九章

One who would take the world and shape it will fail — I see that it cannot be done. The world is a sacred vessel; it cannot be shaped or grasped. To shape is to ruin; to grasp is to lose. Therefore the sage does not shape, so nothing is ruined; does not grasp, so nothing is lost. Some lead, some follow; some blow hot, some cold; some grow strong, some weak; some rise, some fall. Therefore the sage discards excess, extravagance, and extremes.

将欲取天下而为之，吾见其弗得已。夫天下，神器也，非可为者也。为者败之，执者失之。物或行或随，或热或吹，或强或挫，或培或堕。是以圣人去甚、去大、去奢。

Chapter 30 · 第三十章

One who assists the ruler with the Way does not use weapons to dominate the world; such deeds rebound. Where armies camp, thorns grow; after great wars come years of famine. The good achieve their aim and stop; they dare not rely on force. Achieving, they do not boast; do not claim; do not grow arrogant; act only when forced; do not press the advantage. Things that grow strong grow old — this is not the Way; what is not the Way ends early.

以道佐人主，不以兵强于天下。其事好还，师之所居，荆棘生焉。大军之后，必有凶年。善者果而已矣，毋以取强焉。果而毋骄，果而毋矜，果而毋伐，果而毋得已居，是谓果而不强。物壮而老，是谓之不道，不道早已。

Chapter 31 · 第三十一章

Weapons are instruments of ill omen; all things detest them, so those with the Way do not rest on them. At home the gentleman honors the left; in war the right. Weapons are not the gentleman's tools; only when unavoidable does he use them, and calm is best. Victory is no glory; to glorify it is to delight in killing. One who delights in killing cannot gain the world's trust. Auspicious affairs favor the left, inauspicious the right; mourning rites govern the aftermath of battle, and the killing is mourned with tears.

夫兵者，不祥之器也。物或恶之，故有道者不处。君子居则贵左，用兵则贵右。故兵者非君子之器也，不得已而用之，恬淡为上。勿美也，若美之，是乐杀人也。夫乐杀人，不可以得志于天下矣。是以吉事上左，丧事上右；是以偏将军居左，上将军居右，言以丧礼居之也。杀人众，以悲哀莅之；战胜，以丧礼处之。

Chapter 32 · 第三十二章

The Way is eternally nameless; simple and unadorned, though small, none in the world can master it. If rulers could keep it, all things would follow of themselves. Heaven and earth would unite, sweet dew would fall, and the people would be equally at ease without command. Once names are distinguished, know also when to stop; knowing when to stop avoids danger. The Way in the world is like rivers and streams flowing to the sea.

道恒无名，朴虽小，而天下弗敢臣。侯王若能守之，万物将自宾。天地相合，以俞甘露，民莫之令而自均焉。始制有名，名亦既有，夫亦将知止，知止所以不殆。譬道之在天下也，犹小谷之与江海也。

Chapter 33 · 第三十三章

Knowing others is intelligence; knowing oneself is enlightenment. Overcoming others is strength; overcoming oneself is power. Contentment is wealth; perseverance is purpose. Staying in one's place brings endurance; dying without perishing is true longevity.

知人者智也，自知者明也。胜人者有力也，自胜者强也。知足者富也，强行者有志也。不失其所者久也，死而不亡者寿也。

Chapter 34 · 第三十四章

The great Way overflows; it can go left or right. All things depend on it for life, and it does not refuse them; it accomplishes without claiming. It clothes and feeds all things without ruling them; it may be called small. All things return to it without being ruled; it may be called great. Because it never makes itself great, it achieves greatness.

道泛呵，其可左右也。成功遂事而弗名有也，万物归焉而弗为主。则恒无欲也，可名于小；万物归焉而弗为主，可名为大。是以圣人之能成大也，以其终不为大，故能成其大。

Chapter 35 · 第三十五章

Hold to the great image, and the world will come; it comes unharmed, in peace and safety. Music and delicacies detain the passing guest; but the Way spoken aloud is plain and tasteless — looked at, it cannot be seen; listened to, it cannot be heard; used, it cannot be exhausted.

执大象，天下往。往而不害，安平大。乐与饵，过客止。故道之出言也，曰：淡呵其无味也。视之不足见也，听之不足闻也，用之不可既也。

Chapter 36 · 第三十六章

What is to be contracted must first be expanded; what is to be weakened must first be strengthened; what is to be discarded must first be exalted; what is to be taken must first be given. This is called subtle insight. The soft and weak overcome the hard and strong. Fish should not leave the deep; the instruments of the state should not be shown to others.

将欲歙之，必固张之；将欲弱之，必固强之；将欲去之，必固举之；将欲夺之，必固予之。是谓微明。柔弱胜刚强。鱼不可脱于渊，邦之利器不可以示人。

Chapter 37 · 第三十七章

The Way constantly acts without action, and nothing is left undone. If rulers could keep it, all things would transform of themselves. When transformation stirs desire, I would subdue it with nameless simplicity. Nameless simplicity is free of desire; free of desire and still, the world settles of itself.

道恒无为也，侯王能守之，而万物将自化。化而欲作，吾将镇之以无名之朴。镇之以无名之朴，夫将不辱。不辱以静，天地将自正。

Chapter 38 · 第三十八章

Higher virtue is not virtuous; therefore it has virtue. Lower virtue never neglects virtue; therefore it has no virtue. Higher virtue acts without intention; lower virtue acts with intention. Higher benevolence acts without intention; higher righteousness acts with intention. Higher rites act, and when none respond, sleeves are rolled and force applied. Thus, after the Way is lost comes virtue; after virtue, benevolence; after benevolence, righteousness; after righteousness, rites. Rites are the thinning of loyalty and trust, and the beginning of disorder. Foreknowledge is the flower of the Way, and the beginning of folly. Therefore the great man dwells in the thick, not the thin; in the substance, not the flower. He rejects the latter and chooses the former.

上德不德，是以有德；下德不失德，是以无德。上德无为而无以为也；下德为之而有以为。上仁为之而无以为也；上义为之而有以为也。上礼为之而莫之应也，则攘臂而仍之。故失道而后德，失德而后仁，失仁而后义，失义而后礼。夫礼者，忠信之薄也，而乱之首也。前识者，道之华也，而愚之首也。是以大丈夫居其厚，不居其薄；居其实，不居其华。故去彼取此。

Chapter 39 · 第三十九章

Those of old who attained the One: heaven attained it and became clear; earth and became tranquil; spirits and became efficacious; valleys and became full; all things and came to life; kings and the world was ordered. Without clarity, heaven would split; without tranquility, earth would quake; without efficacy, spirits would fade; without fullness, valleys would dry; without life, things would perish; without order, kings would fall. Thus honor takes lowliness as its root; height takes low ground as its foundation. Therefore kings call themselves orphaned, widowed, and unworthy — is this not taking lowliness as root? Is it not so? Hence the highest praise needs no praise. Do not aspire to the luster of jade, but to the hardness of stone.

昔之得一者：天得一以清，地得一以宁，神得一以灵，谷得一以盈，万物得一以生，侯王得一以为天下正。其致之也：天毋已清将恐裂，地毋已宁将恐发，神毋已灵将恐歇，谷毋已盈将恐竭，万物毋已生将恐灭，侯王毋已贵以高将恐蹶。故必贵而以贱为本，必高矣而以下为基。夫是以侯王自谓孤寡不谷，此其贱之本与？非也？故至数誉无誉。是故不欲琭琭若玉，珞珞若石。

Chapter 40 · 第四十章

Reversal is the motion of the Way; weakness is the function of the Way. All things under heaven are born of being; being is born of non-being.

反也者，道之动也；弱也者，道之用也。天下之物生于有，有生于无。

Chapter 41 · 第四十一章

When the highest man hears of the Way, he diligently practices it; the middling man hears and half remembers; the lowest hears and laughs aloud. Without laughter, it would not be the Way. Thus the old sayings: the bright Way seems dim; progress seems retreat; the smooth Way seems rough; higher virtue seems like a valley; great purity seems stained; broad virtue seems insufficient; steadfast virtue seems lax; true substance seems changeable; the great square has no corners; the great vessel is slow to complete; the great sound is scarcely heard; the great image has no shape — the Way is hidden and nameless. Yet the Way alone aids and completes all things.

上士闻道，勤能行之；中士闻道，若存若亡；下士闻道，大笑之。弗笑，不足以为道。是以建言有之曰：明道如昧，进道如退，夷道如类；上德如谷，大白如辱，广德如不足，建德如偷，质真如渝；大方无隅，大器免成，大音希声，大象无形，道褒无名。夫唯道，善始且善成。

Chapter 42 · 第四十二章

The Way gives birth to One; One gives birth to Two; Two gives birth to Three; Three gives birth to all things. All things carry yin and embrace yang, blending their energies into harmony. What people dislike — being orphaned, widowed, unworthy — is what kings call themselves. Things may be diminished and so increased, or increased and so diminished. What others teach, I also teach: the violent and forceful do not die a natural death. I take this as the father of my teaching.

道生一，一生二，二生三，三生万物。万物负阴而抱阳，中气以为和。天下之所恶，唯孤寡不谷，而王公以自名也。物或益之而损，或损之而益。人之所教，亦议而教人。强梁者不得其死，我将以为学父。

Chapter 43 · 第四十三章

The softest thing in the world rides through the hardest. Non-being enters where there is no space. Thus I know the benefit of non-action. Wordless teaching and the benefit of non-action — few in the world attain them.

天下之至柔，驰骋于天下之至坚。无有入于无间，吾是以知无为之有益也。不言之教，无为之益，天下希能及之矣。

Chapter 44 · 第四十四章

Name or body, which is dearer? Body or wealth, which matters more? Gain or loss, which is the greater ill? Great love costs greatly; hoarding invites heavy loss. Knowing contentment avoids disgrace; knowing when to stop avoids peril; thus one endures.

名与身孰亲？身与货孰多？得与亡孰病？甚爱必大费，多藏必厚亡。故知足不辱，知止不殆，可以长久。

Chapter 45 · 第四十五章

Great perfection seems flawed, yet its use never wears out. Great fullness seems empty, yet its use never runs dry. Great straightness seems bent; great skill seems clumsy; great eloquence seems hesitant. Stillness overcomes heat; calm overcomes haste. Perfect clarity is the order of the world.

大成若缺，其用不敝；大盈若盅，其用不穷。大直如诎，大巧如拙，大辩如讷。躁胜寒，静胜热，清静可以为天下正。

Chapter 46 · 第四十六章

When the world has the Way, horses are used for farming; when the world lacks the Way, war horses breed in the suburbs. No calamity is greater than discontent; no fault is greater than wanting more. Therefore, contentment with contentment is lasting contentment.

天下有道，却走马以粪；天下无道，戎马生于郊。罪莫大于可欲，祸莫大于不知足，咎莫憯于欲得。故知足之足，恒足矣。

Chapter 47 · 第四十七章

Without leaving the door, one knows the world; without looking out the window, one sees the Way of heaven. The farther one goes, the less one knows. Therefore the sage knows without traveling, names without seeing, accomplishes without acting.

不出于户，以知天下；不窥于牖，以知天道。其出也弥远，其知也弥少。是以圣人不行而知，不见而名，弗为而成。

Chapter 48 · 第四十八章

In learning, one increases daily; in the Way, one decreases daily. Decrease and decrease again, until one reaches non-action. Through non-action, nothing is left undone. To win the world, act without design; if one has designs, one is not worthy of the world.

为学者日益，闻道者日损。损之又损，以至于无为。无为而无不为。将欲取天下也，恒无事；及其有事也，又不足以取天下矣。

Chapter 49 · 第四十九章

The sage has no fixed heart; the people's heart is his heart. To the good I am good; to the not-good I am also good — thus goodness is attained. To the trusting I am true; to the not-trusting I am also true — thus trust is attained. In the world the sage is reserved, treating all hearts as children's.

圣人恒无心，以百姓之心为心。善者善之，不善者亦善之，得善也；信者信之，不信者亦信之，得信也。圣人之在天下也，歙歙焉，为天下浑心；百姓皆属其耳目，圣人皆孩之。

Chapter 50 · 第五十章

Life comes out and death enters. Of the living, three in ten are on the path of life; three in ten on the path of death; and three in ten who move toward death through clinging to life. Why? Because they cling too hard to life. I have heard that one who masters life walks without meeting rhinos or tigers, enters battle without armor or blade — the rhino finds no place for its horn, the tiger no place for its claw, the soldier no place for his blade. Why? Because he has no place for death.

出生入死。生之徒十有三，死之徒十有三，而民生生，动皆之死地之十有三。夫何故也？以其生生也。盖闻善摄生者，陵行不避兕虎，入军不被甲兵；兕无所投其角，虎无所措其爪，兵无所容其刃。夫何故也？以其无死地焉。

Chapter 51 · 第五十一章

The Way gives them life; virtue rears them; matter shapes them; conditions complete them. Thus all things honor the Way and esteem virtue. This honor is not commanded but constant and natural. So the Way gives life, virtue rears, grows, nurtures, matures, completes, nourishes, and shelters. It gives life without possessing; acts without claiming; raises without dominating. This is the profound virtue.

道生之而德畜之，物形之而器成之。是以万物尊道而贵德。道之尊也，德之贵也，夫莫之爵也，而恒自然也。道生之，德畜之，长之遂之，成而熟之，养之覆之。生而弗有也，为而弗恃也，长而弗宰也。此之谓玄德。

Chapter 52 · 第五十二章

The world has a beginning, which is the mother of the world. Having found the mother, one knows her children; knowing the children, one returns to keeping the mother, and to the end of life one is free of danger. Block the passages and close the doors, and one is never exhausted; open the passages and meddle in affairs, and one is beyond saving. To see the small is enlightenment; to keep the soft is strength. Use the light, return to brightness, leave no harm to the body — this is practicing the constant.

天下有始，以为天下母。既得其母，以知其子；既知其子，复守其母，没身不殆。塞其兑，闭其门，终身不勤；启其兑，济其事，终身不救。见小曰明，守柔曰强。用其光，复归其明，毋遗身殃，是谓袭常。

Chapter 53 · 第五十三章

If I had the least knowledge, walking on the great Way, I would fear only the bypaths. The great Way is smooth, yet people love the side paths. The court is corrupt, the fields overgrown, the granaries empty, yet they wear fine robes, carry sharp swords, gorge on food, and hoard wealth. This is robbery and extravagance — not the Way at all!

使我介然有知，行于大道，唯施是畏。大道甚夷，而民好径。朝甚除，田甚芜，仓甚虚；服文采，带利剑，厌食而货财有余，是谓盗夸。盗夸，非道也哉！

Chapter 54 · 第五十四章

What is well built cannot be uprooted; what is well embraced cannot slip away; descendants will honor it without end. Cultivate it in the self, and virtue is real; in the family, it overflows; in the village, it grows; in the state, it abounds; in the world, it is universal. Thus see the self in the self, the family in the family, the village in the village, the state in the state, the world in the world. How do I know the world is so? By this.

善建者不拔，善抱者不脱，子孙以祭祀不绝。修之身，其德乃真；修之家，其德有余；修之乡，其德乃长；修之国，其德乃丰；修之天下，其德乃溥。以身观身，以家观家，以乡观乡，以邦观邦，以天下观天下。吾何以知天下之然？以此。

Chapter 55 · 第五十五章

One rich in virtue is like a newborn child: poisonous insects do not sting, wild beasts do not seize, birds of prey do not strike. Bones soft and tendons weak, yet the grip is firm; unaware of the union of male and female, yet full of vigor — the perfection of essence. Crying all day without growing hoarse — the perfection of harmony. Knowing harmony is the constant; knowing the constant is enlightenment; forcing life is ill fortune; letting breath serve the heart is force. Things that grow strong grow old — this is not the Way; what is not the Way ends early.

含德之厚者，比于赤子。蜂虿虺蛇弗螫，攫鸟猛兽弗搏。骨弱筋柔而握固。未知牝牡之合而朘怒，精之至也；终日号而不嗄，和之至也。和曰常，知和曰明。益生曰祥，心使气曰强。物壮即老，谓之不道，不道早已。

Chapter 56 · 第五十六章

Those who know do not speak; those who speak do not know. Block the passages, close the doors, blunt the sharp, untangle the knots, soften the glare, merge with the dust — this is called mysterious union. Thus one cannot be made intimate, nor estranged; cannot be benefited, nor harmed; cannot be exalted, nor debased. Therefore one is esteemed by all under heaven.

知者不言，言者不知。塞其兑，闭其门，挫其锐，解其纷，和其光，同其尘，是谓玄同。故不可得而亲，亦不可得而疏；不可得而利，亦不可得而害；不可得而贵，亦不可得而贱。故为天下贵。

Chapter 57 · 第五十七章

Govern the state with rectitude; use war with surprise; win the world with non-action. How do I know? Thus: the more taboos the world has, the poorer the people; the more weapons, the darker the state; the more cleverness, the stranger the things; the more laws, the more thieves. Therefore the sage says: I act without action, and the people transform themselves; I love stillness, and the people correct themselves; I do not meddle, and the people enrich themselves; I have no desires, and the people return to simplicity.

以正治邦，以奇用兵，以无事取天下。吾何以知其然也哉？夫天下多忌讳，而民弥贫；民多利器，而邦滋昏；人多知，而奇物滋起；法令滋章，而盗贼多有。是以圣人之言曰：我无为而民自化，我好静而民自正，我无事而民自富，我欲不欲而民自朴。

Chapter 58 · 第五十八章

When government is lenient, the people are simple; when it is strict, the people are cunning. Misfortune is the shadow of fortune; fortune is the omen of misfortune. Who knows their end? There is no fixed rule: the upright reverts to the strange, goodness reverts to the monstrous. Long has the people been deluded. Therefore the sage is square without cutting, angular without piercing, straight without being harsh, bright without dazzling.

其政闷闷，其民惷惷；其政察察，其民缺缺。祸，福之所倚；福，祸之所伏。孰知其极？其无正也。正复为奇，善复为妖。人之迷也，其日固久矣。是以圣人方而不割，廉而不刿，直而不肆，光而不耀。

Chapter 59 · 第五十九章

In governing people and serving heaven, nothing is better than frugality. Frugality means early submission; early submission means accumulating virtue; with accumulated virtue, nothing is impossible; with nothing impossible, none knows one's limit; when none knows one's limit, one may hold the state; holding the state's root, one endures. This is the Way of deep roots and firm stalks — lasting life and lasting sight.

治人事天，莫若啬。夫唯啬，是以早服；早服是谓重积德。重积德则无不克，无不克则莫知其极；莫知其极，可以有国；有国之母，可以长久。是谓深根固柢、长生久视之道也。

Chapter 60 · 第六十章

Governing a great state is like cooking a small fish. Rule the world with the Way, and its ghosts lose power; not only the ghosts, but the spirits do not harm the people; not only do the spirits not harm the people, the sage does not harm them either. When neither harms the other, virtue flows back to both.

治大邦若烹小鲜。以道莅天下，其鬼不神；非其鬼不神也，其神不伤人也；非其神不伤人也，圣人亦弗伤也。夫两不相伤，故德交归焉。

Chapter 61 · 第六十一章

A great state flows low; it is the confluence of the world, the female of the world. The female often overcomes the male through stillness, taking the lower place. Thus a great state that lowers itself before smaller states wins them; a small state that lowers itself before greater states is won. So some lower themselves to win, others lower themselves and are won. Great states wish only to nurture others; small states wish only to serve. Both get their wish, but the great must take the lower place.

大邦者，下流也，天下之牝也，天下之交也。牝恒以静胜牡，为其静也，故宜为下也。故大邦以下小邦，则取小邦；小邦以下大邦，则取于大邦。故或下以取，或下而取。故大邦者不过欲兼畜人，小邦者不过欲入事人。夫皆得其欲，则大者宜为下。

Chapter 62 · 第六十二章

The Way is the refuge of all things — the treasure of the good, the protection of the not-good. Fine words can win honor; fine conduct can win esteem. If a man is not good, why abandon him? Therefore, in crowning the emperor and appointing ministers, though one bears jade before teams of horses, it is better to sit and offer this Way. The ancients valued it because, seeking, one finds, and, guilty, one is freed. Thus it is the most precious thing under heaven.

道者，万物之注也；善人之宝也，不善人之所保也。美言可以市，尊行可以贺人。人之不善也，何弃之有？故立天子，置三卿，虽有共之璧以先四马，不善坐而进此。古之所以贵此道者何也？不谓求以得，有罪以免与？故为天下贵。

Chapter 63 · 第六十三章

Act without action; work without effort; taste without tasting. Great or small, many or few, repay resentment with virtue. Plan for the difficult while it is easy; do the great while it is small. All difficult things begin easy; all great things begin small. Thus the sage never strives for greatness, and so achieves greatness. Light promises invite little trust; many easy things invite many difficulties. Thus the sage treats everything as difficult, and so meets no difficulty.

为无为，事无事，味无味。大小之多易必多难。是以圣人犹难之，终无难。图难于其易也，为大于其细也。天下之难作于易，天下之大作于细。是以圣人终不为大，故能成其大。夫轻诺必寡信，多易必多难。是以圣人犹难之，故终于无难。

Chapter 64 · 第六十四章

What is at rest is easy to hold; what has not yet appeared is easy to plan for; what is brittle is easy to break; what is small is easy to scatter. Act before it exists; order before disorder arises. A tree a fathom thick grows from a hair; a tower of nine stories rises from a mound of earth; a journey of a thousand miles begins from under one's feet. Those who act ruin; those who grasp lose. Thus the sage acts without acting and so ruins nothing, grasps without grasping and so loses nothing. People often fail just before success; be as careful at the end as at the beginning, and nothing fails. Therefore the sage desires not to desire, does not prize rare goods, learns not to learn, and returns to where the many have gone astray, aiding the natural way of all things without daring to act.

其安也，易持也；其未兆也，易谋也；其脆也，易判也；其微也，易散也。为之于其未有也，治之于其未乱也。合抱之木，生于毫末；九层之台，作于累土；百仞之高，始于足下。为之者败之，执之者失之。是以圣人无为也，故无败也；无执也，故无失也。民之从事也，恒于其成事而败之。故慎终若始，则无败事矣。是以圣人欲不欲，而不贵难得之货；学不学，而复众人之所过；能辅万物之自然，而弗敢为。

Chapter 65 · 第六十五章

The ancient masters of the Way did not enlighten the people with it, but kept them simple. The people are hard to govern because they are too clever. To rule by cleverness is the nation's curse; to rule without it is the nation's blessing. Knowing both is a pattern; constantly keeping the pattern is the mysterious virtue. Profound and far-reaching, it turns back with all things, and so reaches the great harmony.

故曰：为道者，非以明民也，将以愚之也。民之难治也，以其智也。故以智治邦，邦之贼也；以不智治邦，邦之德也。恒知此两者，亦稽式也。恒知稽式，是谓玄德。玄德深矣，远矣，与物反矣，乃至大顺。

Chapter 66 · 第六十六章

Rivers and seas become kings of all valleys because they lie below them. Thus the sage who would be above the people must speak humbly; who would lead them must place himself behind. Thus he dwells above, yet the people are not burdened; leads in front, yet they are not harmed. The world delights in supporting him without wearying. Because he does not contend, none can contend with him.

江海之所以能为百谷王者，以其善下之也，故能为百谷王。是以圣人之欲上民也，必以其言下之；其欲先民也，必以其身后之。故居上而民弗重也，居前而民弗害也。天下乐推而弗厌也。非以其无争与？故天下莫能与争。

Chapter 67 · 第六十七章

All under heaven say my Way is great but seems unlike anything. Precisely because it is great, it seems unlike; if it were like, long since it would be small. I have three treasures; hold and keep them: the first is compassion, the second frugality, the third not daring to be first in the world. Compassion gives courage; frugality gives breadth; not daring to be first makes one the vessel of leadership. Today, abandoning compassion for courage, frugality for breadth, the rear for the front — this is death. Compassion in battle brings victory, in defense security; heaven will save what compassion guards.

天下皆谓我大，大而不肖。夫唯不肖，故能大；若肖，细久矣。我恒有三宝，持而宝之：一曰慈，二曰俭，三曰不敢为天下先。夫慈，故能勇；俭，故能广；不敢为天下先，故能为成器长。今舍其慈，且勇；舍其俭，且广；舍其后，且先；则必死矣。夫慈，以战则胜，以守则固。天将建之，如以慈垣之。

Chapter 68 · 第六十八章

The good commander is not warlike; the good warrior is not angry; the good victor does not contend; the good leader of men is humble. This is the virtue of non-contention, the power of using others, the ultimate of heaven since ancient times.

善为士者不武，善战者不怒，善胜敌者弗与，善用人者为之下。是谓不争之德，是谓用人之力，是谓配天古之极。

Chapter 69 · 第六十九章

In warfare there is a saying: I dare not be the host, but the guest; I dare not advance an inch, but retreat a foot. This is to march without marching, to roll up sleeves without arms, to confront without enemies, to wield weapons without blades. No greater calamity than underestimating the enemy; to underestimate the enemy nearly loses my treasures. Thus when equal forces meet, the mourning side wins.

用兵有言曰：吾不敢为主而为客，吾不敢进寸而退尺。是谓行无行，攘无臂，执无兵，乃无敌矣。祸莫大于无敌，无敌近亡吾宝矣。故抗兵相若，则哀者胜矣。

Chapter 70 · 第七十章

My words are easy to understand, easy to practice, yet none under heaven understand or practice them. Words have their source; affairs have their master. Because they do not know, they do not know me. Few know me; therefore I am precious. Thus the sage wears coarse cloth but holds jade in his heart.

吾言甚易知也，甚易行也；而人莫之能知也，而莫之能行也。言有君，事有宗。夫唯无知也，是以不我知。知我者希，则我贵矣。是以圣人被褐而怀玉。

Chapter 71 · 第七十一章

To know that one does not know is best; not to know yet claim to know is sickness. The sage is not sick because he is sick of this sickness. Being sick of sickness, he is free of it.

知不知，尚矣；不知不知，病矣。是以圣人之不病，以其病病也，是以不病。

Chapter 72 · 第七十二章

When the people do not fear authority, greater authority comes. Do not confine their dwellings; do not oppress their lives. Because he does not oppress, he is not oppressed. Thus the sage knows himself without displaying, loves himself without exalting; he rejects the one and takes the other.

民之不畏威，则大威将至矣。毋狭其所居，毋厌其所生。夫唯弗厌，是以不厌。是以圣人自知而不自见也，自爱而不自贵也。故去彼取此。
